So basically, if what you're really trying to do is prevent free market behavior about hoarding and so on, then I'm telling you, there's Law 4054. There are two articles of Law 4054. Article 4 says that companies who work in the same line of work can't make a price deal. It means you can't set up a price cartel, and if you set it up, you'll be punished. The 6th article also states that a law firm cannot abuse its situation. It can't cause a shortage, for example. And so, yes, you have a law on setting up retail trade, but I'll tell you this: when this law is being made, it's already generally accepted that it's mixed with the coverage area of Law 4054, and so there's a problem that needs to be solved, you're thinking of it as a mechanism that companies don't act, and you're just, sir -- of course, the whole community's problem is this -- in order to prevent prices from going up. Well, let me ask you something else: let's say, let's say the oil is sold for $100 a liter, and I sell it, and I raised it to $150. Can I take it off? Yeah, I can, there's no legal obstacle, so there's no law against me raising it to 150 lira. But, dear friends, when I raise it to 150, do you think I can sell my property? I can't. Why? Because everyone's selling it for $100. So there's one issue that you don't think enough about or explain about the price behavior of companies.
